1160. JOHN DYER and EDWARD LONG were indicted for stealing, on the 27th of August, two sheets, value 12 s.; four handkerchiefs, value 9 s.; and two books, value 5 s., the goods of Joseph Cory.
DYER pleaded
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined One Month.
JOSEPH CADBY. I am a constable of the Foundling. On the 27th of August, about three o'clock in the afternoon, I saw the two prisoners together in Guildford-street. I watched them for half an hour, and then lost sight of Dyer, and in half an hour afterwards I lost sight of Long. I remained on the spot until about six o'clock, and saw them in company again; I still watched them, and stopped them on suspicion. Upon searching them, I found two books and a handkerchief on Long. The place where I stopped him was half a mile from the prosecutor's. A parcel of duplicates were found on Dyer, and also the handkerchiefs and books found on Long. Dyer said at first, the property belonged to him, and afterwards that it belonged to his brother. Long said Dyer gave him the books to carry for him.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did Long deny giving Dyer the books - A. No; he said he lent them to him to read, and the handkerchief also.
FRANCIS FAGAN. I am a constable. On the 27th of August, about six o'clock in the evening, I saw the prisoners in Great Coram-street. I followed them to Guildford-street, and there took them into custody. I found a duplicate of two shirts and a handkerchief on Dyer, and a handkerchief on his neck, which Mr. Corry claimed. Dyer admitted giving Long the books.
GEORGE SHEPHERD. I am servant to Mr. Armstrong, pawnbroker, Baldwin's-gardens. On the 26th of August a shawl was pawned - on the 2d, a sheet for six shillings - on the 5th, another, and on the 15th, another - they were all pawned by Dyer.
MR. JOSEPH CORRY. I live in Everett-street, Brunswick-square. I know nothing of this robbery. I might have been out of town at the time - the property is mine - I had let my house to a tailor, for whom Dyer worked. I never saw Long there.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-192]
1161. HARRIET DAVENPORT was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of August, twenty sheets, value 30 s., the goods of Thomas Kennedy.
THOMAS KENNEDY. I keep a lodging house in St. Giles's. I saw these sheets safe the morning before they were stolen. I have eight rooms, and five or six beds in some rooms. I missed ten pair next morning. I saw the prisoner about eleven o'clock, and in consequence of information had her taken. I found nine pair at Rodnams, in Seven-dials - she took me there - my name is on them at full length.
JANE REECE. I am shopwoman to Mr. Rodnam; the prisoner and a man brought these sheets to our house. I bought them at the rate of 2 d. a lb. I did not open them, nor know how many there were. She offered them to me as rags - they weighed 30 lbs. I paid her the money, as she appeared to belong to them. If I had opened the bundle, I should not have bought them at all - she kept the money herself.
EDWARD HEDGES. I am beadle of Bloomsbury. The prisoner came to Marlborough-street, in consequence of two girls being in custody, who were suspected of this - they were liberated, and she detained.
Prisoner's Defence. I met a man who asked me where I could sell them. I took him to this shop, but never had the money.
GUILTY. Aged 38.
Confined One Year.
[trialAccount t18230910-193]
1162. MARY ELLIOTT was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of August, 20 lbs. of rope, value 18 s., the goods of William Vincent.
WILLIAM VINCENT. I am a rope-maker, and carry on business at Stepney. On the 29th of August, about eleven o'clock in the day, I saw this rope safe in my ground - it weighed 20 lbs. I missed it, and saw it afterwards at Shadwell, where the prisoner was in custody. She came backwards and forwards to my house - she has been a great many years in a very respectable business.
DANIEL DARBY. I am a rope maker, I work for Mr. Vincent - I saw the prisoner bringing it off the premises on the 29th of August, between six and seven o'clock in the evening - I did not stop her, supposing she had my master's permission to bring it. I informed my master next morning.
RICHARD LEE. I am a cord-winder. On the last Saturday in August, the prisoner came to me with this rope, and asked if she might leave it with me until the afternoon. I live nearly a mile from her; she did not call for it, but on Sunday morning, her husband and son came for it.
WILLIAM FORSTER. I am an officer of Lambeth-street. Brown, who is now dead, gave the rope into my care.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner pleaded Distress.
GUILTY. Aged 42.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18230910-194]
1163. SAMUEL GLOVER was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of July, a jacket, value 2 s., and a pair of trowsers, value 2 s., the goods of Daniel Dundway.
DANIEL DUNDWAY. I live in Hampshire. I came to town to look for work, I lodged at Mr. Turner's, at Highgate, the prisoner slept in the same bed. I undressed and went to bed about eight o'clock in the evening of the 14th of July; the prisoner went to bed at the same time - four other persons slept in the room; I awoke at two o'clock and he was gone, the rest remained. I missed my jacket and trowsers, and found them about a fortnight ago - he was taken into custody.
JAMES TURNER. The prosecutor and prisoner lodged at my house. The prisoner came about the 7th of July; he appeared a labourer. Dundway came to lodge there a day or two before - he was very ill, and when I went to take him his breakfast, he said his clothes were stolen, and 3 s. 6 d. The prisoner was apprehended at Totteridge.
BARNARD FITZPATRICK. I am a labourer at Highgate. On the 14th of July, I slept in the same house, but not in the same room. I heard the door open and shut, but do not know what it was about - I hallooed out, but no answer was given. I saw the prisoner on the day he was taken, and asked him what made him take the money - he said there was very little money to take.
THOMAS BRETT. I am a constable. I took him in charge, he said he was drinking at the time. I found the trowsers on John Riley, who is gone to Ireland, and the jacket on Lowe.
THOMAS ROACH. I am a labouring man. On Tuesday morning the prisoner came to Finchley-common, and got a job with my master - he had the jacket and trowsers on then which are now produced. I saw him exchanging the trowsers with Riley, he sold the jacket to Lowe for 1 s. 3 d.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18230910-195]
1164. MARY HAMILTON was indicted for stealing, on the 14th June, a coat, value 2 s.; a pair of shoes, value 3 s.; a shift, value 3 s.; a gown, value 1 s.; a shawl, value 1 s.; a pair of stays, value 1 s.; six caps, value 2 s.; a pair of stockings, value 6 d., and three handkerchiefs, value 18 d., the goods of Mary Garton, widow.
MARY GARTON. I am a widow, and live at St. James's poor-house. On the 14th of June, between nine and ten o'clock at night, I was at the Duke of Wellington, public-house, Princes-street, and had these things in a bundle; I was enquiring for a lodging; the prisoner came in; I knew her before. She said she would give me a lodging, and took the bundle from me, saying, she would carry it for me; we walked together a little way. I went to leave a basket at a house, and when I came out she was gone - I have found nothing. I met her seven weeks after, and charged her with it, she said she had none of my things, and used bad language - she went into a wine-vaults, at the corner of Crown-street, and by going out at another door, escaped me.
EDMUND PEPPER. I am a constable. On the 18th of August, I took the prisoner in charge. She and the prosecutrix had both been paupers - she refused to say where she lodged, or what she had done with the things.
Prisoner's Defence. She was in liquor. I said, if she would go with me, I would provide for her; but she got dancing with the men. I lost sight of her, and never touched her bundle.
EDMUND PEPPER. She said the prosecutrix was drunk, but said nothing about her being with the men.
MARY GARTON. I was not drunk.
GUILTY. Aged 58.
Confined One Year.
[trialAccount t18230910-196]
1165. HENRY HARMAN was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of September, twenty-eight yards of calico, value 3 s., the goods of John Smith.
JOHN SMITH. I am a linen-draper, and live in Sloane-street. On the 8th of September I lost this printed calico from outside the door. It was put out in the morning between six and seven o'clock, and was the top piece of a pile. Two ladies gave me information. I went out, and caught sight of the prisoner running in New-street. I collared him in the Brompton-road, and this calico fell from him. He begged forgiveness.
JOHN CANNING. I am a constable. I took charge of the prisoner with the property.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. It was not on the pile, but laid on the pavement. I was out of work.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Confined Three Weeks.
[trialAccount t18230910-197]
1166. WILLIAM HAWKINS was indicted for embezzlement.
ANN MARIA RATCLIFF. I am a single woman, and by trade a baker. The prisoner has been in my service ten months or a year, and received money for me, which he should account for immediately as he came home. Langhorn was a customer of mine; she owed me money. I asked the prisoner if she had paid him; he said, No. I discharged him on the 24th of August, 1822. I saw Langhorn next week. He was apprehended in July last, and has never accounted to me for money received of her.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. He was very active in getting you customers - A. Yes; he had 8 s. a week. I advanced his wages (as he got customers) to 1 l. I never asked him to make up money which they did not pay. He gave me the money as being paid by the customers. There was a dispute between him and my brother, in consequence of which he left. I never offered to give up the prosecution for money. I do not think I ever said, if his friends paid me I would give it up. I told his father I would not hurt him if he would give security for the money he had received. He would not do that unless I received him into my service again, and stopped it out of his wages.
SARAH LANGHORN. I live in Union-street, Somers'-town. I dealt with the prosecutor. On the 24th of June, 1822, I paid the prisoner 3 s. To the best of my knowledge I gave him 3 s. I must have given him a shilling. The week after the prisoner left the prosecutrixes service, a bill was sent me amounting to 2 l. 9 s. I had paid it all but 4 s.
Cross-examined. Q. Might not you have given him a half-crown, a sixpence, and halfpence - A. I gave him no halfpence. I remember paying him.
BENJAMIN SCHOFIELD. I apprehended the prisoner on the 10th of July, at a baker's shop in the Borough-road. I told him I took him for embezzling 30 l. from the prosecutrix. He confessed his guilt, and afterwards asked how much she charged him with. He then said it was between 20 and 30 l., for he kept a book, but it was not so much as 30 l.
Prisoner's Defence. When I engaged with her I was to have half out of every ten loaves sold. I was induced to give credit, and have paid bills out of my own pocket. I became indebted to her in consequence of giving credit, and intended to pay her all. An account of it was made out. I had words with her brother, and left. She offered to take it by instalments.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Confined One Year.
[trialAccount t18230910-198]
1167. JOHN PORTER was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of August, a till, value 1 s.; thirty pennypieces, and sixty halfpence, the property of Joseph Wiltshire.
JOSEPH WILTSHIRE. I keep the White Horse, public-house, Mile End . On the 19th of August, about eleven o'clock, I went out, returned in an hour, and the till and money were gone; 5 s. or 6 s. worth of copper were in it.
ELIZABETH MANNING. I live opposite to the prosecutor's. On the 19th of August, between eleven and twelve o'clock, I saw the prisoner come out with a box under his arm. He was a neighbour, and I thought nothing of it. On the Friday following I heard Wiltshire had been robbed. There were three of them came out, but the prisoner was the one who had the box. (Looks at the till); it was a box like that.
WILLIAM GIBBS. I am a constable. On the 24th of August, in consequence of information, I took the prisoner, and told him it was for robbing Mr. Wiltshire of his till - he denied it; I found nothing on him. I apprehended him at his father's house; he was denied to me, but I heard the yard door go down - I went backwards, and found him in the privy. The till was brought to Wiltshire, about half an hour after.
THOMAS COOPER. I accompanied Gibbs - his statement is correct.
JOSEPH WILTSHIRE. The till is mine. A young woman brought it to me.
Prisoner's Defence. I know nothing of it. The witnesses have said that right or wrong I shall leave the country.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18230910-199]
1168. PROSPER MILLER was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of July, a coat, value 5 s., the goods of Thomas Thompson.
THOMAS THOMPSON. I am a carter, and live at Turnham-green. On the 25th of July, about half-past three o'clock in the morning, the cart was at the top of James-street, Covent-garden - I had just unloaded it; the coat was then safe. I missed it in half an hour. The prisoner was taken about four o'clock.
THOMAS BARTLETT. I am patrol of Drury-lane. I saw the prisoner about four o'clock in the morning, with another, in Great Queen-street - one of them had a bundle. As soon as they saw me, they turned into Queen-street, and from there into Great Wild-street. Before that, theywere coming toward me - I ran round to meet them; they set off as hard as they could; I followed them through several courts, sprung my rattle, and took the prisoner with the coat on his back - he said he found it; he pulled it off, and threw it down. As we went along to the watch-house, he offered me the coat if I would let him go. I made enquiry, and found Thompson, who claimed it.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I picked it up in the road, and was going to the market to ask who it belonged to.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18230910-200]
1169. WILLIAM PATMORE was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of July, a sheet, value 2 s., the goods of John Hill.
JOHN HILL. I keep the Cross Keys, St. John-street . On the 31st of July, I lost a sheet, which had come round some meat - I missed a variety of things. The prisoner was employed about the yard, and knew of my loss, but said nothing. In consequence of information I received, I gave him in charge.
THOMAS THOMPSON. I am a constable. On the 31st of July, I apprehended the prisoner in the yard - I told him it was for taking a meat sheet; he said he was in the stable, and did take it, and gave it to Allensby to make aprons of.
ELIZABETH ALLENSBY. I live in New-court, St. Peter's-lane. I know the prisoner by sight. On the 30th of July, he gave me this sheet to make into aprons for him - it was in four pieces, and he gave me eleven yards of tape to make them with. My husband worked for Mr. Hill.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I found a piece of wrapper, which I thought of no consequence, and took it to make aprons, as it is customary to find porters aprons.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Confined One Year and Publicly Whipped.
[trialAccount t18230910-201]
London Cases, Before Mr. Common Sergeant.
1170. LYDIA STEVENS and THOMAS KNIGHT, were indicted for misdemeanours.
MR. LAW conducted the prosecution.
WILLIAM COLTON. On the 11th of July, I was in Sun-street, and saw the prisoners go by me about twelve o'clock, towards Bishopsgate-street. I followed them in company for three quarters of a mile, they separated at Adams's shop; Stevens went in, and Knight waited at the corner, about twenty yards off, and when Stevens came out, I went in, and from what I heard I watched them - they went together to Morgan's milk shop. I saw Knight hand her something; he stood in a corner about forty yards off, while she went in, bought some eggs and got change - then they joined company, and went away together. I went into the shop and received a sixpence, which they marked before they delivered it. They then went to Bullock's shop, near Little Britain; Knight stood at the corner, while she went in and came out with a sheet of writing paper - I went in, Miss Bullock marked a sixpence and gave it to me. Reardon was with me, we took them into custody. I asked Knight what he had in his hand, he said nothing; I forced it open, and found a bad sixpence; I found ninepence in halfpence, and some potatoes in his hat, and some in his coat, and two or three good shillings about him. I found the eggs and paper in Stevens's hand, but no halfpence; she had no bonnet or shawl on. I produce the three sixpences.
DANIEL REARDON. I am a plaisterer, and live in Grenville-street, Somers-town. I was with the last witness, and have heard his statement, it is correct.
Prisoner KNIGHT. Q. What did you give Colton at the Compter door - A. Nothing. The prisoner was going to throw something away, and Colton took a sixpence out of his hand.
MARY ADAMS. I am the wife of Robert Adams, we live in Throgmorton-street. On the 11th of July, the prisoner came into the shop, between two and three o'clock, for two raspberry tarts, and begged me to give her fresh ones, as they were for a sick child. She opened her hand, and said,
"I have only got these three-halfpence and a sixpence, which you must give me change for." On looking at it, I saw it was bad, and returned it; she said, she would return in half an hour, if I would put them aside. She went out, and Colton came, and I told him what had occurred.
MARTHA GEORGE. I am servant to Mr. Morgan, cow-keeper, to Fore-street. I saw the female prisoner on Friday, the 11th of July; she came for two eggs, which came to twopence. She said, she had only three halfpence, and I must change a sixpence, which I received from her; I took it up to my mistress, who gave me fourpence, which I gave her. I saw her deliver the same sixpence to Colton, it was marked.
JEMIMA MORGAN. I am the wife of William Morgan, Fore-street. George was my servant; she brought me a sixpence, I gave her fourpence. I kept it separate until Colton came, when I gave it him, after marking it.
ELIZABETH BULLOCK. I live with my father, who is a stationer, in Little Britain. On the 11th of July, a woman came into the shop; I cannot say whether it was the prisoner; she asked for a sheet of black edged letter paper, which I gave her; she gave me a sixpence, I gave her fivepence change. Colton came in directly after, I gave him the sixpence; he marked it before I parted with it; this is it (looking at it.)
MR. CALEB EDWARD POWELL, assistant to the Solicitor for the Mint, proved the sixpences to be all counterfeit, and of one die.
STEVENS'S Defence. I met Knight, and told him to come with me while I fetched some errands, which he did; I received no money from him, I did not know it was bad.
KNIGHT'S Defence. I had no bad money about me, and gave the woman none.
GUILTY.
Confined One Year, and to find sureties.
[trialAccount t18230910-202]
1171. SARAH ASH, GEORGE SINGLETON, and HENRY HEDLEY were indicted for misdemeanours.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-203]
Middlesex Cases, Third Jury.
1172. EDWARD POWELL was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of July, a looking-glass, value 25 s., the goods of James Paterson.
WILLIAM SIMPSON PATERSON. I am the son of JamesPaterson. On the 16th of July, about eight o'clock, my father was coming from the parlour, and called out
"Stop thief! there is a glass stolen." I ran into Bedford-place, and saw the glass on the pavement broken, and the prisoner in the hands of the beadle. I had seen it safe ten minutes before. We live in Vernon-place.
EDWARD HEDGES. I am beadle. On the 16th of July I saw the prisoner running round Bloomsbury-square into Bedford-place, and there I took him; he did not attempt to get away - he put the glass down carefully - I think it was broken by accident.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18230910-204]
1173. THOMAS STONE was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of August, a watch-key, value 7 s., the goods of Abraham Harris and Lewis Harris.
ABRAHAM HARRIS. I am in partnership with Lewis Harris; we are pawnbrokers, and live in the Strand. On Monday, the 18th of August, about seven o'clock in the morning, I was called up, and found the prisoner in custody in the passage, and two gold watch-keys gone from the window. The shutter was loose, the pin stolen, the glass broken, and part of the shutter cut away, so as to enable a person to put a wire in. The prisoner was the last boy in the shop on Saturday night; this was Monday.
EDWARD LOMAS. I work for Mr. Ashton, who lives opposite to the prosecutors. On the 18th of August, about a quarter before seven o'clock, I saw the prisoner working at the shutters; he had a pitcher stood by his side - a little girl came up, and he took it up and went away - I went over and took him as he was going into a wine-vaults, and said,
"What have you been doing at the shutters?" he said,
"What shutters?" I found a wire in his hand, which was bloody. I gave him in charge, and found the shutter had been cut and the glass broken.
THOMAS PARKER. I am servant to Mr. Ashton. I saw the prisoner with the wire, working at the shutters; and as he went away. I called to Lomas to stop him. I saw him throw a key into the gutter - it was picked up in my presence and given to Lomas, who secured him.
THOMAS THURNAN. Early on the 18th of August I was in the Strand, and saw a key lying on the ground; I stopped to pick it up, but another man took it. The prisoner was in custody of Lomas at the time.
JOHN WITHEY. I am a street-keeper, and took him in charge - I have the key and wire - I found a bent nail in his coat pocket; there was also a ticket attached to the key which was picked up under the window. The house appeared to have been broken open in the night, for there was the mark of a crow bar under the window.
Prisoner's Defence. They say it was done at five o'clock; I was not out of bed at the time.
GUILTY. Aged 12.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18230910-205]
1174. GEORGE THOMAS was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of July, a gown, value 2 s., the goods of Joseph Adams.
JOSEPH ADAMS. I live in Field-lane - my wife keeps a mangle. On the afternoon of the 12th of July I was taking some linen home, and had three gowns with it. The other woman told me something was gone out of my basket, and pointed out the prisoner; I followed him and took the gown from under his coat.
Prisoner. Q. Have you got the woman here - A. No; he said,
"You old *** I will knock your eye out if you don't let let me go;" I could not detain him, he had so many palls.
JOHN BARNSLEY. I took the prisoner in charge the morning after the robbery; he said it was only a joke.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-206]
1175. RICHARD TYLER was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of August, three sovereigns, a half sovereign, and ten shillings, the monies of James James.
JAMES JAMES. I am a saddler, and live in Joseph-street, Brunswick-square. The prisoner was in my service - the money stated in the indictment was given him to get some leather from Jones and Co. in Piccadilly; he went out, and I did not see him again till he was taken, which was on the 9th of September.
WILLIAM BARNES. I found the prisoner, and gave him in charge for this transaction; he said he was very sorry - that his master had behaved extremely well to him, but it was now too late - and acknowledged taking it.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18230910-207]
1176. THOMAS NAPPER was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of August, a pair of boots, value 10 s., the goods of William Vinicombe, privately in his shop.
FRANCES VINICOMBE. I am the wife of William Vinicombe; we live in Tabernacle Walk. On the 28th of August, about a quarter to three o'clock, I saw the prisoner with these boots in his hands, walking fast away from the shop - I went out after him - he dropped them and ran off, but was stopped - they had hung up about a yard inside the door; I saw them safe ten minutes before.
SAMUEL BRIDGES. I am a constable. I received him in charge.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18230910-208]
1177. ANN WILSON and ELIZA BOWERS were indicted for stealing, on the 2d of August, a blanket, value 3 s.; two sheets, value 4 s.; two knives, value 6 d.; two forks, value 3 d.; a bason, value 4 d.; a milk-jug, value 2 d.; and a towel, value 3 d., the goods of William Rowe, in a lodging-room.
PAMELA ROWE. I am the wife of William Rowe, and live in Chapel-street, Westminster. The prisoners lodged in our second floor, back room; they took it together, furnished, on Saturday, the 3d of August. On Sunday, Bowers went out with a dish; I asked what was in it - she said only a table cloth, and went out saying she was going to her aunt's to dinner. Wilson had left the day before; neither of them returned. I missed these things, which were let with the lodging.
JOHN SIMPSON. I am a pawnbroker. A blanket was pawned with me on the 9th of August, in the name of A. Wilson - I do not know who by.
LANCELOT WILD. I am a pawnbroker's servant. On the 8th of August, a shawl was pawned in the name of A. Wilson - I do not know who by.
THOMAS HARTLEY. I am a pawnbroker - I have a sheet pawned on the 5th of August in the name of Ann Head - I do not know the person.
JOSEPH WEEKS. I am an officer. I took the prisoners on the 12th of August; they said they lodged at Lambeth, and gave me the key of their lodging. I went there, but found an officer had broken the door open. I found the duplicates of the property produced; also some crockery and other things, which the prosecutor claims.
WILSON - GUILTY. Aged 15.
BOWERS - GUILTY. Aged 14.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18230910-209]
1178. HENRY WILSON and AMELIA WILSON were indicted for stealing, on the 31st of August, a bed, value 2 l.; a bolster, value 3 s.; a pillow, value 3 s.; a blanket, value 10 s.; a quilt, value 10 s.; two sheets, value 4 s.; a trunk, value 4 s.; six pictures, value 6 s.; a set of fire-irons, value 3 s.; a looking-glass, value 3 s.; and four chairs, value 6 s., the goods of William Hannaford, in a lodging-room.
ELIZABETH HANNAFORD. I am the wife of William Hannaford. The female prisoner took a lodging at my house for herself and husband. They both came and lived together for four months. The lodgings were furnished. On the 31st of August, the articles stated in the indictment were missed; they still remained in the lodging - we had them secured. The man said, he certainly knew they were gone, but did not say how they went.
ELIZABETH BRITAIN. I bought some pictures and a jug of the female prisoner; also four chairs at four different times.
THOMAS KEARNEY. I was fetched, and took the prisoner in charge. I went to Britain's, in Clement's-lane, and found two pictures., a trunk, and a jug; the woman said she would replace them if she would give time.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
AMELIA WILSON 'S Defence. I intended to replace them - she knew the things were parted with three months before.
ELIZABETH HANNAFORD. I did not know they were gone till Sunday.
AMELIA WILSON - GUILTY. Aged 29.
Transported for Seven Years.
HENRY WILSON - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-210]
1179. RACHAEL SIMMONDS was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of July, a bolster, value 5 s.; and two blankets, value 2 s., the goods of Sebrino Guzeer, in a lodging-room.
SEBRINO GUZEER. I am a seaman, and keep a house in Gray's-inn-lane. The prisoner took a furnished room of me on the 8th of July.
SARAH GUZEER. I am the wife of the prosecutor. I let the prisoner a furnished lodging on the 8th of July. She remained for a week. I asked her for an iron I had lent her. She shut the door in my face, and said she had none. I asked her repeatedly for it - she would not open the door. I said if she did not, I would fetch a constable. While I went, she left. I found the door open, and these things missing.
JOHN TYE. I am a pawnbroker. I produce a blanket and pillow pawned by the prisoner, one on the 15th of July and the other on the 18th, in the name of Summers.
WILLIAM CREED. The prisoner pawned a blanket with me on the 16th of July, which I produce.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GEORGE STRIPLING. I am an officer, she was given into my charge. I saw the duplicates of these things found on her, she is frequently out of her mind.
GUILTY. Aged 66.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18230910-211]
1180. JOHN GIBSON was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of July, a sheet, value 5 s., the goods of Henry Rogers, in a lodging-room.
MARY ROGERS. I am the wife of Henry Rogers. I let the prisoner a furnished lodging more than a year ago; he is son-in-law to one Buckley, who had it before him. He was apprehended this day four weeks, and I then missed a sheet and blanket off another bed, which I had let to him with the room.
HENRY JOEL. I am servant to Mr. Beauchamp, pawnbroker, Holborn. I produce a sheet pawned on the 14th of July, in the name of Gibson, I cannot say who by.
WILLIAM MARSH. I produce the duplicate of this sheet. I apprehended the prisoner, and he gave me the ticket.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I pawned the sheet, nothing more, it was done through extreme distress.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18230910-212]
1181. RICHARD BUCKLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of August, a bed, value 1 l., the goods of Henry Rogers, in a lodging-room.
MARY ROGERS. I let the room to the prisoner before Gibson - I missed nothing while he had it. I found this bed pawned on the 6th of August, 1822, at that time he was my lodger.
WILLIAM MARSH. I am an officer. I apprehended the prisoner, and found the duplicate of the bed upon him.
WILLIAM CREED. This bed was pawned with me by a woman.
Prisoner's Defence. It was my full intention to redeem it, - I should have done it in a fortnight - I was distressed.
GUILTY. Aged 63.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18230910-213]
1182. CHARLES STOCK was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of July, four pair of clasps, value 1 l. 1 s., the goods of William Hinchloe, from his person.
WILLIAM HINCHLOE. I am a tailor, and live in Gray's Inn-lane. The prisoner was in my service; I missed four steel clasps from my closet; they were safe on Thursday the 10th of July, between eight and ten o'clock. I missed them on the Saturday following, and have not found them, he had absconded. I met him on Sunday morning, on Saffron-hill, he trembled when he saw me; I said I wanted him to go with me. I charged him with stealing the clasps, as nobody but him could have done it, and insisted on his telling the truth. I neither threatened norpromised him - he said he had sold them to Mr. Lyon, of High-street, St. Giles's, for 5 s. I went there with him; Lyon denied ever seeing him.
CHARLES READ. I am an officer. I received him in charge, he voluntarily confessed that he took them, and sold them to Lyons - Lyons denied it.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18230910-214]
1183. NICHOLAS THORP was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of July, a suit of boys clothes, value 5 s.; three shirts, value 2 s.; a night gown, value 6 d.; a cap, value 2 d., and a pinafore, value 6 d., the goods of Michael Clokeley, from the person of Amy Abbott .
AMY ABBOTT. I am servant to Mr. Sayer, of Tower-street. On the 25th of July, about eight o'clock at night, I was going home from Whitechapel with John Clokely, who is seven years old; his father's name is Michael. Opposite Whitechapel church, a bundle was suddenly snatched from my arm by a man, it contained the property stated in the indictment; and he immediately ran up a court to the right. I saw it in half an hour at Spitalfields watch-house.
THOMAS BROWN. I am a watchman of Whitechapel. On the 25th of August, I saw the prisoner pass the end of Montague-street into Wentworth-street, with a bundle under his right arm; I followed him, and when I came up with him, he was in the hands of an officer; I first saw him about two hundred yards from Whitechapel church. Almond said, he had dropped a bundle, and I found it about thirty yards from where it was taken.
THOMAS ALMOND, On the 25th of July, about ten o'clock, I stopped the prisoner in Wentworth-street; he dropped the bundle when he saw me, and ran off; he fell, and I took him. The prosecutrix claimed it.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. A man ran by me and knocked me in the mud; then Almond laid hold of me and picked up the bundle; it was never in my possession.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18230910-215]
TWELFTH DAY, TUESDAY, SEPTEMBER 22.
Middlesex Cases, Fourth Jury,
Before Mr. Common Sergeant.
1184. BARTHOLOMEW MORAN was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of August, a pair of half-boots, value 4 s. 6 d., the goods of Jonathan Ward.
JONATHAN WARD. Elizabeth Ward is my daughter; she is nearly eleven years old - here are a pair of half-boots, which are my property.
ELIZABETH WARD. On the 29th of August, about the middle of the day, I saw the prisoner by my father's door with two or three more. I was coming to the door, and saw them looking at the shoes which hung at the doorpost - they took them down, and hung them up again, and then I went away.
ISSAC CLARK. I live opposite Ward, at Highgate. On the 29th of August, about two o'clock, I saw four boys at his door; the prisoner was one of them. I did not see him take the boots; my son said they were stolen. I saw the prisoner with something bulky in his apron; I went over to Ward's, and saw them crossing the road, and took hold of one of the others - the prisoner then ran up a little passage, and came out without the bulk - I went to him and asked what he had done with the shoes - I found them in the passage, behind the door, where he went up.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-216]
1185. HENRY GODFREY, HENRY ARCHBOLD, and AUGUSTUS FREDERICK BOARDS were indicted for stealing, on the 18th of July, a saw, value 15 s., the goods of John Biggs and William Holmes; and three wedges, value 3 s.; an axe, value 2 s.; a pealing iron, value 6 d.; a beetle, value 6 d., the goods of the said John Biggs; and an axe, value 2 s.; a mattock, value 2 s., and a peeling iron, value 6 d., the goods of the said William Holmes; and a chisel, value 1 s., the goods of Thomas Bolton; and RICHARD JORDAN and ABRAHAM BANKS, were indicted for feloniously receiving the same, knowing them to be stolen.
JOHN BIGGS. I am a labourer. I was at work on Mr. Bower's premises, felling some timber; Holmes and Porter were also at work there, about the end of May or the beginning of June; the saw was the joint property of Holmes and myself. I lost other tools; I was working at Coneyhatch.
WILLIAM HOLMES. I know the saw, mattock, one axe, and anger. Biggs and I bought the saw between us - they were stolen out of a shed, but I do not know when.
THOMAS BOLTON. I missed a chisel, very much like one that is here, but I cannot swear to it - I locked it up about the middle of May, in a shed, with other tools - in Honeyburn's shed.
THOMAS PEART. On Sunday morning, the 13th of June, I saw Boards and Godfrey, between four and five o'clock; I was a long time with them, and went to Mr. Honeyburn's, in Coneyhatch-lane - Boards pulled a spar away from the shed and got in, and handed out these tools to us - we hid them in Mr. Robinson's hedge, about two fields off. I went there with the constable about a fortnight afterwards, I shewed them the place and they found the beetle.
Cross-examined by MR. WALFORD. Q. When were you taken up - A. One Monday, a fortnight after - I told Dr. Owen of a good many robberies which I had been concerned in. I have come from the House of Correction now.
JOHN MORRISON. I am a horse patrol. On the 29th of July, about ten o'clock in the evening, I saw Banks and Jordan in the Swan and Two Necks, public-house, near Finchley Common; they said they found some tools in a field, and wished to give them up to me, which they did; I left them at a public-house, and they went away.
THOMAS PEART re-examined. I saw Jordan about a fortnight after the robbery. As Boards and I were going towards the Swan we met him. Jordan said,
"Have you planted them?" He said, No; it was wet that night, and he could not. Jordan told him to plant them near the road, so as to get them handy. I asked Boards what they were worth; he said 1 l. Jordan said he would not give that for them. Boards had said before, that he had got somebody to buy them.
THOMAS BRETT. I received the three prisoners in charge for a highway robbery. I went with Peart into Robinson's field, but found nothing except the beetle - we found the others at the Swan.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-217]
1186. HENRY GODFREY, HENRY ARCHBOLD, and AUGUSTUS FREDERICK BOARDS were again indicted for stealing, on the 25th of July, 6 lbs. of bacon, value 2 s., the goods of Jane Richards.
There being no evidence, except the accomplice, Peart, the prisoners were
ACQUITTED.
[trialAccount t18230910-218]
Before Mr. Recorder.
1187. CHARLOTTE THOMAS was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of July, a pillow, value 2 s.; a pillow-case, value 6 d.; five knives, value 1 s.; six forks, value 1 s.; a blanket, value 6 d.; two pair of stockings, value 1 s., and two half-handkerchiefs, value 1 s., the goods of John Gifford, her master.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
MR. JOHN GIFFORD. I live on Muswellhill. The prisoner was in my service, as cook, up to the 22d of July - she had been with us more than a year - after she was gone we missed the articles stated in the indictment; among others, I found she lodged with one Glascott, in the neighbourhood - I went there with an officer, and found her at home in a room up one pair of stairs - she said she knew nothing about it; search was made in her presence; a pillow and case found on her bed, and inside the pillow, among the feathers, were five knives and six forks - they were parlour knives, and had never been used - we missed more - the initials were nearly picked out of the pillowcase; but the thread holes were visible. I found a blanket and two pair of stockings, with the marks on; I believe I had never worn them; I never gave her any stockings; they were tied up in a pair of stockings belonging to a man who had formerly lived with me. A cravat of mine had been cut in two; I found half of it on her neck with my name on it at full length.
Cross-examined by Mr. ALLEY. Q. You took her with a good character from a friend of your's - A. No, she came from Wales; she broke her collar-bone some time ago.
ELIZA GLASSCOTT. I live on Muswell-hill; the prisoner lodged on my first floor in July or August; and lodged there when the prosecutor came - she lodged there alone.
JOHN CONWAY. I assisted in the search, and found the property.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. About four months before I left her service, I wanted a flannel for a house-cloth; she told me to take the ironing blanket - I kept the blanket, and cut up some old petticoats of my own. I broke my collar-bone, and had a pillow of my own, and took the wrong one away. The prosecutor said before I left, that he would do me all the injury he could on account of my leaving his service to be married.
MR. GIFFORD. I never used any such language.
ANN HORTON. I am servant to Mr. Gifford. I never knew that the prisoner had a pillow of her own.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Judgment Respited.
[trialAccount t18230910-219]
1188. WILLIAM MOLE was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of August, in the dwelling-house of James Bennett, a hat, value 10 s.; four sovereigns; and 20 s., his property.
JAMES BENNETT. I am a hatter, and live at No. 28, Oxford-street - the prisoner was shopman at another shop of mine, No. 31. On the 4th of August, about six o'clock in the evening, I returned from the city, and was told he had gone out with a new hat on, and left his own behind. I found the till empty, which contained four sovereigns and 20 s. in the morning. He was the only person there except the errand boy. I went to his lodings - he was not there - I went there again about two o'clock in the morning, and found him; the hat was there; the constable charged him with stealing the hat and money; he said it was all very true. Four sovereigns and some silver were found in his pocket.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. In the morning you left 5 l. in case change should be wanted - A. Yes; he managed the shop. I saw his wife when I first went to his lodgings. She fetched him, and next time I found him at home. I have every reason to suppose he was intoxicated, and in that state he might take the money, meaning to take care of it.
WILLIAM BARKER. I am errand-boy, and am thirteen years old. The prisoner went out about five in the afternoon with a new hat on, and left his own behind. I said,
"Why, you have got one of master's new hats on." He said, Yes; he was going to shew a gentleman the shape of it. I saw him at the desk about a quarter of an hour before he left, and when my master came home he missed the money. I saw he appeared the worse for liquor. He told me to get the tea ready, and he should return in ten minutes, but never returned. We shut up at nine o'clock.
ROBERT NEEDHAM. I am a constable. I went with the prosecutor to the prisoner's lodgings, and found him in bed. The hat was on the drawer, which Bennet claimed. I said he must go to the watch-house. I said I took him for robbing his master. I did not mention money or any thing. He was very much intoxicated, and said he very well knew all about it. I found four sovereigns and six shillings in his pocket.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-220]
1189. RICHARD NORRIS was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of September, five pieces of horse-hides, value 10 s., the goods of Noah Slee and Jonah Slee.
JOHN SHIELDS. I am a Thames Police officer. On the 10th of September, about half-past seven o'clock in the evening, I met the prisoner at the top of King-street, Tower-hill, with a basket of skins. I asked where he bought them from. He said from Mr. Slee, of Bermondsey. I asked where he was going to take them. He said at Mr. Connel's, near Shadwell. I asked if he had a note, or bill. He said, No; that Mr. Connel was just gone on, and he supposed he had got it. I said I should go with him to Connell's. He went, and shewed me the house. I went in and asked Connell, a shoe-maker, if he knew the prisoner, or any thing about the skins. He said, No; but a woman came in, and asked what was the matter. I asked if she knew anything of it. She said, Yes. Connell said he knew nothing of them; and then she said so too. He looked at Connell, and said,
"You know I was to bring them here." I went over to the prosecutor's, leaving him in custody.
Cross-examined by Mr. PHILLIPS. Q. He told you at once where he came from - A. Yes.
JOHN THORP. I am an officer. I kept the prisoner in custody. I asked him how long Connell had been at his master's to buy the skins. He said about two hours. He said Mr. Slee was to allow him overtime for carrying them. I told him it was wrong; and asked him who sent him with them. He said the clerk sent him with them, but he did not know his name. They were quite wet, as if just out of the pit.
JOSIAH SLEE. I am in partnership with Noah Slee. The prisoner was in our service. The skins are not in a saleable state. I did not employ him to carry them.
EDWARD HEMMINWAY. I am clerk to Messrs. Slee. I never gave the prisoner the skins.
EVAN WILLIAMS. I am the prosecutor's clerk, and did not give him the skins. My master has no other clerk.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 29.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18230910-221]
1190. JANE VANCE was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of August, five caps, value 5 s.; a ring, value 4 s.; a shirt, value 3 s.; a gown-body, value 2 s.; a half-handkerchief, value 2 d., and a piece of ribbon, value 6 d., the goods of George Huggett.
SARAH HUGGETT. I am wife of George Huggett. We live in the Commercial-road. The prisoner was acquainted with me, and used to do needle-work in my front room. She was backward and forward every day. On the 9th of August I missed these things, and went with a constable to her lodgings, at Stepney. She has a blind mother, who she supports by her labour. I think she was in distress. I found three caps, a gown-body, and handkerchief there. She was crying all the time. I found a sheet in pawn.
GEORGE RAVENOR. I am a pawnbroker, and live at Ratcliff, The prisoner pawned a shirt for 4 s.
ELIZABETH MANN. I live in New Gravel-lane. The prisoner gave me two caps.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
CHARLES READ. I found the prisoner at her lodgings. There was every appearance of distress there.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18230910-222]
1191. HENRY WARD was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of September, two sheets, value 8 s.; a blanket, value 5 s., and a looking-glass, value 1 s., the goods of James Labdon, in a lodging-room let by him to the prisoner.
The lodging being let jointly to the prisoner and another person, he was
ACQUITTED.
[trialAccount t18230910-223]
1192. WILLIAM WARE was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of August, a bugle, value 50 s., the goods of William Dale.
WILLIAM DALE. I am a musical-instrument seller, and live in the Poultry. On the 14th of August, at night, this bugle was in the shop. I missed it next morning about ten o'clock; and on the 21st found the prisoner at Benton's, in Holborn, and took him.
JAMES BENTON. I am a pawnbroker. On the 15th of August, between eleven and twelve o'clock at noon, the prisoner pawned this bugle in the name of Ware, for 30 s. I never saw him before, but am certain of him. He came again, in about a week, to sell the duplicate to a person. Mr. Dale was there with the officer, and took him.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
WILLIAM CULVERWELL. I am shopman to Mr. Dale I saw the bugle safe, near the door, at nine o'clock in the evening. A person must have got into the shop to get it. I missed it a few minutes after nine o'clock. I had seen a man about the shop for an hour and a half. He was of the same size and appearance as the prisoner.
GEORGE SMITH. I keep a sale-shop in the New-cut. On the 20th of August, the prisoner offered to sell me the duplicate of a bugle for 10 s. He took me next morning to Benton's to see it.
JOHN LIMBRIC. I went with Mr. Dale to the pawnbrokers. The prisoner said he bought it of a man in the street, and did not know where he lived.
Prisoner's Defence. I became acquainted with a man at the Jerusalem Coffee-house. He brought me this bugle, covered with a handkerchief, and told me to pawn it for 30 s. He said he was going to sea, and the duplicate would be of no use to him. I might have it.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-224]
1193. THOMAS WIGGINS was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of July, a handkerchief, value 2 s. 6 d., the goods of Abraham Luck, from the person of Martha Luck .
MARTHA LUCK, a child, twelve years of age, not appearing to understand the nature of an oath, was not examined.
ABRAHAM LUCK. I am father of Martha Luck, and live in Charlotte-mews, Bloomsbury. I know nothing of this circumstance.
DANIEL HONE. I am headborough of St. Giles's. On the 29th of July, about five o'clock in the afternoon, I heard the cry of Stop thief! in Castle-street, opposite my own door - I immediately looked out, and saw the prisoner running with a handkerchief in his hand; he ran about thirty yards, then threw it down, and the little girl ran to pick it up. I saw the prisoner stopped, and only lost sight of him as he turned the corner - he was the only person running. I brought him back to where the girl had picked the handkerchief up. She said in his presence that she had it loose round her neck, with her hand on it, and he took it off - he said he picked it up.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was in Museum-street, and saw it on the ground; I picked it up, and heard the cry of Stop thief! dropped it, and ran to see where the thief was.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18230910-225]
1194. CHARLOTTE SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of August, a watch, value 40 s., and a seal, value 5 s., the goods of David Read, from his person.
DAVID READ. I live in Edmund-street, Battle-bridge, and work for an upholdster. On the 7th of August, about eleven o'clock in the morning, I was waiting at the corner of George-street, St. Giles's, for my brother. The prisoner and another girl came up, and asked for drink; they teazed me, and at last I took them into a house, and gave them a glass each - they were strangers. I came out again, and stood waiting there, and did not miss my watch for half anhour - I was sober. I found the prisoner in custody with it, on the 11th.
LANCELOT WILD. I am shopman to Mr. Wells, pawnbroker, of High-street, St. Giles's. On the 7th of August, a little before twelve o'clock, the prisoner came to pawn the watch, and said it was her own - I detained it, and gave her in charge. The prosecutor's father-in-law's name, Thomas Parker, was round the dial - she gave me the name of Parkhouse, and said her husband had it made for him five years before, and that was his name round it.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was sent to pawn it.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18230910-226]
1195. RICHARD HURLING was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of June, a hat, value 5 s., the goods of Richard Baker, from the person of Elizabeth, his wife.
ELIZABETH BAKER. I am the wife of Richard Baker. a gunsmith; we live in Chicksand-street. On the 28th of June. About ten o'clock at night I was in Whitechapel, going to meet my husband; I had fetched this hat by his order, as he wished to have it - it was in my hand; the prisoner came behind me, and said,
"Mistress, I will buy that hat of you, and give you 1 s. for it." I said it was not for sale; he said, he would give 1 s. 6 d.; I said it was not for sale, and stood by a window for him to pass me; he then turned round short, snatched it from me, and ran off with it. I called stop thief! and he was stopped - he dropped it. I saw him in custody in about five minutes - he appeared to be drunk. I understand he bears an excellent character.
JOHN DUNGATE. I am a patrol. I heard the cry, and took the prisoner in the Angel and Crown, public-house; in about two minutes a child picked the hat up fifty yards off in the way he ran. - he seemed very much intoxicated.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was intoxicated, and knew nothing about it.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18230910-227]
1196. TIMOTHY CONNELL was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of July, a watch, value 2 l., the goods of Charles Dawson, from his person.
CHARLES DAWSON. I am a hair-dresser, and live at Cheshunt. On the 21st of July, I was in Shoreditch, going home, and was rather intoxicated, I missed my watch at the end of Shoreditch; it was safe five minutes before. I had not noticed the prisoner near me. I gave information, and found the prisoner at Worship-street, a week after with it.
Cross-examined by MR. WALFORD. Q. Were not some ladies walking with you - A. Not to my knowledge. I went to a house with some - but I knew them before; they had overtaken me.
THOMAS MILLER. I am a pawnbroker. On the 4th of August, the prisoner offered this watch in pawn; but having heard it was stolen, I detained it - he was a customer, and I did not detain him - he lived at the back of my house,
JOHN MANCE. I am a constable, the prisoner appeared at the office to claim this watch - he was sworn, and said he bought it at Bristol, and had had it twelve months. After it was identified; he said his wife had given it him five weeks ago.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-228]
1197. WILLIAM MULLINS was indicted for embezzlement.
WILLIAM STOKES. I am a cheesemonger, and live in Lisson-grove - the prisoner was three or four months in my service, and entrusted to receive money for me, which he should pay me every day. I gave him in charge on the 14th of August, and told him it was for receiving money, and giving no account of it - he said nothing. I had no character with him.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did he not live fellow shopman with you for years before - A. Yes; I had a dispute with him after he came into my service.
Q. Did he not say he had 1 s. 3 d. in his pocket, and could not recollect who he received it of - A. He did at the office - it might be a mistake.
JOHN DYSON. I went to Stokes and bought eggs of the prisoner - he brought 4 lbs. of butter home that day, and I then paid him 5 s. 1 1/2 d. in all, this was about one o'clock; I have his bill and receipt; Stokes called about three.
JOHN STAPLES. I am an officer. About eight o'clock in the evening I took him in charge - he said at the office that he had discovered he had money over, and would return it - but it was a mistake.
WILLIAM STOKES. I received none of this bill; my wife had part of it.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-229]
1198. JOHN CHARD was indicted for embezzlement.
MR. ALLEY conducted the prosecution.
BENJAMIN WARREN. I am in partnership with Robert Warren; we are blacking makers, and live in the Strand; the prisoner was in our service, and entrusted to receive money in the shop - his duty was to ring a bell immediately he received money, that I might come down and take it from him. On the 3d of September, about half-past seven o'clock in the morning, he rang the bell - I went down, and he gave me 1 s., which I produce, and said it was for a shilling bottle of blacking. I saw James Stark two or three hours after, and learnt from him that he had paid 6 s. for six bottles; the prisoner had been home to breakfast before Stark called. I got a constable, and told him he had received 6 s., and only given me 1 s.; he said he gave me all he had received.
Cross-examined by MR. PRENDERGAST. Q. Was it part of his employment to receive money - A. It was; my brother was out of town at the time.
JAMES STARK, JUN. I live with my father, who is a baker. I went to Mr. Warren's and bought six bottles of blacking of the prisoner, and paid him two half-crowns and a shilling, which I received from my father; I did not know that they were marked.
Cross-examined, A. There were several persons in the shop - Q. Yes; they had black and dirty faces; but I am sure of the prisoner - I pointed him out as soon as I entered the shop - I knew him directly.
JOHN BIRCHALL. I am an officer. On the 3d of September, about half past eleven o'clock in the morning, I was fetched to Messrs. Warren's - all the men were calledinto the warehouse; the prisoner was picked out by this boy from ten or fifteen others; and Mr. Warren gave him in charge. I found 1 s. 2 1/2 d. on him not marked.
ANN BURN. I live in Charles-street; the prisoner lodges with me; he paid me a half crown between eight and nine o'clock on the morning he was taken; I gave the same to Herring.
DAVID HERRING. I am an officer. I produce it; it has a mark on it.
JAMES STARK, SEN. I know this half-crown and shilling to be what I gave my son with another half crown, to buy six bottles of blacking. I did not mark them; but my children had rubbed them with nails, and scratched them and several more on the night previous. I am positive to them by the scratches.
Cross-examined. Q. If asked by any one what money you gave the boy, could you describe the place where the scratches were - A. Yes; I could point it out from among fifty. I do not know on which side they were scratched; my son had no money of his own.
MR. WARREN re-examined. He should ring the bell when the customer arrives. But if I am not down, he would take the money.
Prisoner's Defence. I served two or three customers, and gave him the money.
MR. WARREN. He paid me no other money on that morning.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18230910-230]
1199. GEORGE LANE was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of July, 28 lbs. of wool, value 28 s., the goods of John Claxon.
MR. WALFORD conducted the prosecution.
GEORGE WAGER. I am servant to John Claxon, a farmer, of Clapper's-green, Edmonton. We were shearing from May till the end of June; the wool was put into a loft by the side of a barn. I missed some several times; one Wood had been employed by the bailiff; he lived with one Pinto - the prisoner lodged in the same house. I missed wool in July, and put some on a hurdle on Wednesday morning, the 16th, between ten and eleven o'clock, and missed it on the 17th about six in the morning - there was a fleece among it with particular marks on it - that was gone; and in consequence of information I went to Chaplin's house; he shewed me some wool, which was part of what I had laid on the hurdle; the loft was locked up; and I found somebody had pushed the board-up to get to it.
Cross-examined by Mr. PHILLIPS. Q. The board required to be moved to get to it - Q. Yes; it was pushed in. The prisoner was not in my master's employ - but I knew him. Wood was taken, but has escaped.
HENRY CHAPLIN. I live at Cheshunt, about seven miles from the prosecutor's, and am a dealer in wool. On the 17th of July last, about six o'clock in the morning, I bought about 141 lbs. of wool from Wood, for 5 l. 13 s. - the prisoner was with him - they brought it on a horse and cart with the name of Bushby on it. I paid Wood the money.
Cross-examined. Q. All you know is that the prisoner was with the person who sold it you - A. Yes; I never paid the prisoner for any - he helped to unload it - Wood made the bargain. Wood had often brought wool before; Lane only came twice with him, and appeared to be his servant.
JOSEPH GIBSON. I am a constable of Edmonton. I apprehended Lane, on the Monday, at Pinto's, where Wood lodged - I found about a pound of wool, in a bag in the closet, in Lane's room, and found a bed tick doubled up, under the bed, and some little pieces of wool inside it, as if it had contained wool. Lane's wife and children lived in the same room. Wood is quite a little boy, about fourteen years old, and very little for his age.
WILLIAM WAGER. I am bailiff to John Claxton. On the morning of the 17th, I saw Wood and Lane together, in a small pony cart, a little after ten o'clock in the morning, coming towards Edmonton, with empty sacks, and the bed tick in the cart.
HENRY CHAPLIN. I do not know the bed tick - part of it was in a tick.
COURT. Q. Why, Wood is only a boy - A. He appeared to me about eighteen years old, and a full grown man.
GEORGE WAGER. Wood was quite a lad - nobody could take him for a man; he might appear sixteen or eighteen years old. I know the fleece by the tie up, and ochre. Wood had left us at the time it was stolen; he had left us for four months; but came backwards and forwards at times.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.

1200. GEORGE KENNING was indicted for feloniously receiving, on the 21st of August, 50,000 wooden staves, value 100 l., the goods of James Wilkins, well knowing them to have been stolen.
The prosecutor having delivered a regular invoice for the goods in question, the Court ruled that they could not be feloniously stolen.
NOT GUILTY.

1201. JAMES STEWART was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of August, a handkerchief, value 2 s., the goods of Henry Hiatt, from his person.
HENRY HIATT. On the 17th of August, about seven o'clock in the evening, I was in Old-street Road with my wife; two boys went before me, I felt a kick at my heels, and a tug at my pocket; I turned round and saw the prisoner drop my handkerchief; I saw it fall out of his hand.
Q. You told the Magistrate you saw it laying on the ground - A. I saw it fall from his hand, nobody else was near me: he denied taking it.
THOMAS EAGLES. I am a constable of Shoreditch. Hiatt brought the prisoner to me with the handkerchief, and said the boy took it from his pocket, he said nothing to it.
Prisoner's Defence. Two boys who were behind the gentleman went from him. I went forward; he turned round, caught hold of me, and accused me of this - he declared to the people, at a barber's shop, that he could not say I did it.
HENRY HIATT, I did not say so.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-233]
1202. ELIZABETH HUNTER was indicted for stealing,on the 5th of July, a petticoat, value 2 s., the goods of Sarah Ann Williams.
SECOND COUNT, stating them to belong to Michael John Davis.
HANNAH DAVIS. I am the wife of John Davis. The prisoner came into our service on the 28th of January, and on the Wednesday following, I gave her clothes to wash; she went out, returned crying, and said she had heard her father was dead. On the Saturday, I gave her leave to go to her brother for half an hour, and she never returned; I missed some petticoats I had given her to wash; she called on the Saturday following, I charged her with it; she declared she had never seen them, and that I never gave her them to wash. Mrs. Williams who was present, claimed the petticoat she had on.
SARAH ANN WILLIAMS. I was visiting at Mr. Davis's, my petticoat was in the wash; I found it on the prisoner's person, the day she called.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
ANDREW ROBERTS. I am an officer; the prosecutrix described the petticoat before she saw it.
Prisoner's Defence. I was destitute of clothes, and took it.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18230910-234]
1203. WILLIAM COLLETT was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of August, a fixture, (i.e.) a copper, value 1 l., belonging to James Brown, and fixed to his dwelling-house.
JOHN EWIN. I am a watchman, of Mile-end. On the 8th of August, about half-past four o'clock in the morning I stopped the prisoner in White Horse-lane, about one hundred yards from the prosecutor's, with this copper; he said, he kept a cook shop in the Borough, and being in debt, was getting his property away as well as he could.
Cross-examined by MR. BRODRICK. Q. You took him to the watch-house, and he was allowed to go away - A. I believe he was.
ROBERT LEWIS. I am constable of the night. Ewin brought the prisoner to my house, about a quarter past five o'clock in the morning; thinking his story might be correct, as two of the watchmen knew him, I detained the copper, and let him go. Next morning, his father-in-law came to inquire about it, and he was taken again.
JOHN STONE. I apprehended him on the 9th, at his own door, in Dog-row; we knew where he lived.
MARY EYERS. I am servant to James Brown, and know this copper to be his. I have cleaned it for three years, and know it by a mark of soap suds round it, which was there at the time I missed it, at seven o'clock in the morning on the 7th of August; it was taken out of the wash-house window.
Cross-examined. Q. Do you swear to it by the soap suds - A. Yes, and its general appearance - my master lives in Crown-row, Bethnal-green.
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.

1205. SAMUEL JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of August, a coal-scuttle, value 15 s., the goods of Joseph Tunnell.
JOSEPH TUNNELL. I live in Ironmonger-row, Old-street. On the 13th of August, about a quarter to seven o'clock, I heard the cry of Stop thief! and a neighbour brought the scuttle to me. I then ran out, and found a gentleman bringing the prisoner back. It was safe in my parlour the night before.
JOHN BASWICK. On the 13th of August, about ten minutes to seven o'clock, I saw the prisoner and another young man walking about three doors from Tunnell's. I saw one of them lift the window up and take the scuttle out, and give it to the prisoner, who ran away with it. I ran after him, calling Stop thief! he threw it down, and I picked it up. Mitchell stopped him without losing sight of him.
ROBERT MITCHELL. I was going up Waterloo-street, and heard a cry of Stop thief! and stopped the prisoner, who was running before the rest.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Fourteen Days.

1206. THOMAS JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of July, four sheets, value 8 s.; six pair of stockings, value 6 s.; three handkerchiefs, value 5 s.; a shawl, value 18 d.; a waistcoat, value 2 s.; a pair of boots, value 3 s., and a frock, value 18 d., the goods of William Mason.
WILLIAM MASON. I am a watchman. On Saturday morning, the 5th of July, I was on duty in Sloane-street, Chelsea, and on entering my room, about twenty minutes past five o'clock, I found a dirty shirt, a handkerchief, and an old pair of shoes on the floor, and went to the prisoner's mother - these things belong to him.
LUCY MASON. I am the prosecutor's wife - we live in College-street, Chelsea. On Saturday, the 5th of July, about six o'clock in the morning, I missed all the linen off the horse, which I had ironed the night before. The prisoner had lodged with us, and came again that night; he slept in the parlour, and was gone when I got up.
Cross-examined by MR. BRODRICK. Q. Was he in liquor the night before - A. I do not think he was - he left his old things behind.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
THOMAS PACE. On the 5th of July, about a quarter past six o'clock in the morning, I saw the prisoner in Dartmouth-street, Westminster, with these things in a bundle; he walked into Great Queen-street, and there I stopped him; he said he had a shirt, a pair of stockings, and a handkerchief; and I found he had several more things, and took him to the watch-house.
Prisoner's Defence. I was very much intoxicated, and do not know whether I had a bundle or not. I brought this one out, thinking it mine, but when I came to my senses, I found I had it, and was going to take it back.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18230910-238]
1207. MARY PRICE was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of July, a duck, value 18 d., the goods of John Sanders.
JOHN SANDERS. I live in the Commercial-road. On the 30th of July I missed a duck, about two o'clock. I hadkilled it that morning. I saw it again about three, and knew it.
THOMAS SMITH. I am servant to Mr. Sanders. On the 30th of July, about two o'clock in the afternoon, the prisoner came to the window and asked the price of a piece of bacon. My mistress told her it was 6 d.; she went away, and we missed the duck off the board. I overtook her, and asked if she had got it - she said, No. I felt it between two cloths in her apron. I followed her, and she threw it at me and it went into a ditch. I picked it up, and followed her to Globe-row, where she was taken.
KING GEORGE. I am a constable. I took the prisoner into custody at three o'clock, by desire of Smith, who gave me the duck.
Prisoner's Defence. I never saw the duck - he brought a parcel of fellows after me. I told him I had nothing belonging to him.
GUILTY. Aged 47.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18230910-239]
1208. JOHN SHARP was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of June, a brass cock, value 10 s., the goods of Henry Spencer and Thomas Hodgson, his masters.
JAMES GARDENER. I am foreman to Henry Spencer and Thomas Hodgson - the prisoner was in their service. I only know the property.
JOSHUA IVORY. On the 20th of June, I saw the prisoner in Rose-street, with this cock under his coat. I asked what he had got; he said a cock, that he was taking to Carnaby-market, and brought it from Phoenix-street - I took him back, and met two persons fifty yards off, who ran away. Sharp said,
"I will set down the cock, and fetch them back;" I would not let him - he then said he brought it from the warehouse, and that Elliott gave it him.
Prisoner's Defence. I had 1 s. 6 d. a day, and after work if there were any jobs they gave them me to do. Elliott brought me this cock, to take to Carnaby-market, and when the watchman took me, he ran away.
JAMES GARDENER. Elliott was our warehouseman, and absconded immediately this occurred. I think he did give it to him.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-240]
1209. ELIZA ABLETT was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of August, a coat, value 9 s., the goods of Thomas Dyer.
THOMAS DYER. I live in Orchard-street, Westminster. On the 7th of August, I lost a coat, and found it in pawn.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
HENRY RAY. I am apprenticed to Mr. Barnett, pawnbroker. The prisoner pawned this coat in the name of Jones.
ELIZA SECKER. I rent the house Dyer lives in. I saw the prisoner go out, with a coat or jacket under her arm, on the evening of the 6th of August.
MARY DYER. On the 6th of August, I missed my husband's coat, from the side of the bed - the prisoner was in the room that day. I did not see her go out; but when she was gone I missed it.
Prisoner. Q. Were there not other people in the room - A. Only another person, who was discharged at Queen-square.
Prisoner's Defence. I was half an hour in the room - another woman came in, about buying old clothes; Cooley, who was there, went out; but I never saw the coat till the woman who had been about old clothes offered me 6 d. to pawn it.
GUILTY. Aged 33.
Confined One Month.

1210. JOHN DENNISON was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of May, a table, value 4 s., the goods of Thomas Hemmings, the younger.
THOMAS HEMMINGS. On the 28th of May, I employed the prisoner to carry a table to Tyler's-court, Berwick-street, Soho - I carried another to the same place; we left them both at Harding's; I went for them a day or two after, and found one of them gone. I did not authorize him to fetch it - I had given him a shilling and a pint of beer.
MARY HARDING. One day in May the prisoner brought this table - he came back in a few minutes, and said all the other things would be fetched away, and he took the table out of the yard.
HENRY DAY. I bought the table of the prisoner, about the end of May, for 3 s. 5 d.
Prisoner's Defence. I had nothing to give my children food.
GUILTY. Aged 46.

1210. MARY EDMUNDS was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of August, two sheets, value 4 s.; two pillows, value 4 s., and a pair of bellows, value 2 s., the goods of William Smith, in a lodging room.
WILLIAM SMITH. About the 1st of August I let the prisoner a furnished lodging, and in about six days I suspected her, and asked for the key - she said her husband had got it - at last she gave it to me; I said I thought she had made away with the sheets and pillows; she said she had pawned them, and took me to the pawnbrokers; one of them gave up a sheet, and the other a sheet and pillow.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
THOMAS KINGSTON. The prisoner pawned a pillow with me.
Prisoner's Defence. If he had let me stop till Saturday, I should have taken them out.
GUILTY. Aged 40.

1212. MARY ANN HELPS was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of August, a watch, value 20 s.; a chain, value 6 s.; a seal. value 7 s., and a key, value 2 s., the goods of John Willson.
MARGARET WILLSON. I am the wife of John Willson, who lives at Mile-end. I have known the prisoner some time, but had not seen her for six months before this, she was servant in the neighbourhood. On the 21st of August, about half-past ten o'clock in the morning, she called, and said she would stop with me a few hours, if I had no objection - she dined with me, and said she was very ill; I offered her my bed to lay on, which she did. I said, if she was not down at four o'clock, I would call her. She came down at three, I made her tea - she drank one cup, I poured her out another, ran up stairs, and immediately missed my husband's watch from the bed-side, which was safe when she went into the room - when I camedown, she was gone - had left the street door open, and not drank her tea.
THOMAS DREW. I apprehended her on the 22d of August, she said she had pawned the watch in Shoreditch. I named a shop at the corner of an alley - she said she had pawned it there for 25 s., in the name of Anderson, and I found it next day in pawn, in Turnmill-street, in the name of Lawson.
GEORGE HARVEY. I am shopman to Mr. Cross, Turnmill-street. On the 21st of August, in the afternoon, the prisoner pawned the watch for 25 s.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Seven Years.

1213. JOSEPH HEWLING was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of August, a watch, value 50 s., the goods of Robert Brain.
ROBERT BRAIN. On the 27th of August, I was at the Red Cross, public-house, Barbican, the prisoner came in, I told him I had broken my watch - he said the main-spring was broken, and if so, he would put it in for 3 s. 6 d., and if not, he would do it for 6 d; that he would go home to my house and take it to pieces, which he did - and said, he had no tools to put it together, and would take it home and bring it in the morning - but I saw no more of him till he was taken. I knew where he lived.
LEONARD MATTHEWS. I am a pawnbroker. On the 28th of August, the prisoner pawned this watch.
The prisoner in his defence said, he had to go into Berkshire, and took the liberty to pawn it, intending to redeem it.
NOT GUILTY.

THIRTEENTH DAY. WEDNSDAY, SEPTEMBER. 23.
Middlesex Cases, Fourth Jury, Before Mr. Arabin.
1214. JOHN PICKERING was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of July, two live tame pidgeons, price 15 s., the property of Robert Coe.
ROBERT COE. I live in Church-street, Bethnal-green, and am a weaver. I borrowed a pair of pidgeons of Mr. Meux for breeding, and lost them on the 21st of July. I had one of them three weeks, and knew them well; they were carriers - I kept them in my garret - I missed them about six o'clock in the morning, of the 21st of July; they were safe the night before - they were loose in the garret. I found the tiles broken sufficient for a hand to be put in and undo the trap-door. I found one the same day at Baxter's, and the other next morning at Turner's, and knew them well.
Cross-examined by MR. WALFORD. Q. I am told there is a practice of trapping pigeons in the neighbourhood - A. Yes; I do not keep a trap - they call me the cat-killer. My pidgeons could not get out - the prisoner is a tradesman's son in the neighbourhood - he does not keep pidgeons.
MARY BAXTER. I live in Osborne-street, Whitechapel, and keep a bird-shop. The prisoner brought me a white pidgeon; I gave him 1 s. for it - Coe afterwards claimed it.
Q. Are they not worth 15 s. a pair - A. They may be; but my husband was not at home - I did not know the value.
MARY TURNER. I keep a bird-shop in Commercial-road. I bought a carrier pidgeon of the prisoner for 1 s. 6 d.
JOSEPH BROOK. I am an officer. I apprehended him; he said he had sold a pidgeon to Baxter, and no more.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-246]
1215. RICHARD TIBBLE was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of August, a watch, value 10 s., the goods of Richard Baxter.
MARY BAXTER. I am the wife of Richard Baxter; we live in Osborne-street, Whitechapel; the prisoner came to my shop two or three days running; being out of employ I set him him to feed my birds. On Saturday evening, before he went away, I sent him up with a parrot into the room where this watch was. I missed it next morning; it hung by the fire-place. I saw it at the office on Monday.
WILLIAM BELCHER. I am servant to Price, a pawnbroker, of Wentworth-street. On Saturday evening, about eight o'clock, the prisoner came to pawn this watch for 12 s., and said he came from his mother; I said he must send his mother, and he said she was ill. I sent him to fetch his father, and detained the watch, he did not return.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 11.
Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18230910-247]
1216. WILLIAM WHEATLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of June, 100 lbs. of potatoes, value 3 s., and a bushel of oats, value 2 s., the goods of Thomas Life.
THOMAS LIFE. I am a farmer, and live at West Ham; the prisoner was in my service as waggoner. On the 26th of June, I sent him to town with a load of potatoes, about 2 1/2 ton to be sold, to make up money for rent.
GEORGE ELLIS. I am an officer. On the 26th of June, between one and two o'clock in the morning, I was on duty at Mile-end, and heard a noise by the Bell and Mackarel, public-house, and saw the prisoner coming from that direction; we went up to the palings; the prisoner was on this side of the pales - I did not see him do anything myself. I went after him in consequence of what my partner said, and asked him what he had thrown over the pales; he said, it was corn he was allowed by his master to feed his horses. I asked if there was anything else in the sack - he said,
"No;" I said,
"Well, we will go and see what it contains," and as we went along, he said, there was a few potatoes which he dug out of his own garden, and had promised them to the hostler; he said afterwards, that he picked the sack up in the road. I found it contained three pecks of oats, and 50 or 60 lbs. of potatoes.
DANIEL HALEY. I am a Bow-street patrol. I saw the prisoner coming from the paling of the public-house. got over, and found the sack of potatoes and oats. Ellis brought him back. He said it was allowed him by his master for the horses, and he was going to leave it there till he returned, as he generally fed there, and he brought the potatoes for the hostler, as he had done him several favours. That the sack was his own. I asked if his masterhad any waggon on the road. He then said he found the sack altogether as it was, and supposed it must have dropped from his master's waggon, which was gone on, and he left it there to be owned.
Cross-examined. Q. Did he say where he got the potatoes - A. He said he got them from his own home. I do not recollect his saying he had dog them himself.
THOMAS THOMPSON. I work for Mr. Life. I know this sack. I held it up for the prisoner to put corn into two or taken three days before he was. He put two sieves of corn in for the horses.
THOMAS LIFE. I never permitted him to take corn or any thing to town. The horses only go four miles, and we only allow them a little hay. I am certain the potatoes are mine. I went to his garden, and there was not six potatoes dug up. There is not room in his garden to grow these. I am certain of the corn.
Cross-examined. Q. You do not mean to swear to corn - A. Yes; it is grown in marsh land; there is no corn like it in England. I have a sample of the bulk.
RICHARD BARNETT. I thrash for the prosecutor, and believe these oats to he his, but cannot swear to them.
Cross-examined. Q. No man can swear to oats - A. No.
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18230910-248]
Before Mr. Recorder.
