[trialAccount t18230910-314]
1284. WILLIAM LONG was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of August, a watch, value 30 s., the goods of Samuel Warburton Mitchell, from the person of James Alexander Mitchell .
JAMES ALEXANDER MITCHELL. I am the son of Samuel Warburton > Mitchell. On the 14th of August, between eight and nine o'clock in the evening, I was going along Hackney-road. The prisoner came up, and snatched my watch out of my fob, and ran down Weymouth-terrace. I gave an alarm, and he was stopped before I lost sight of him. I am sure he is the man. The watch was picked up on the spot where he was taken, near the Duke of Wellington, public-house.
Cross-examined by MR. ALLEY. Q. What sort of a night was it - A. Not particularly light; there was nobody else about - he ran about a hundred yards.
MARIA MITCHELL. I was with my brother. A man came from the road and snatched his watch - the case immediately dropped - I picked it up. The prisoner was stopped - but I cannot speak to him.
THOMAS FIELD. I keep the Duke of Wellington, public-house. An alarm was given. I ran out, and next morning I found the watch over Mr. Lambeth's wall, under which he was stopped.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GEORGE SMITH. I am an officer, and received him in charge.
Prisoner's Defence. I solemnly declare my innocence. I was going to see my aunt at Haggerston. I heard an alarm, and pursued the thief in the fields, fell down, and was taken.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18230910-315]
1285. ELLEN ARNOLD was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of July, two half-crowns, fourteen shillings, and four sixpences, the monies of Alexander Smith, her master.
EMILY BARNES. I am bar-maid to Mr. Alexander Smith, a publican. On the 13th of July, this bag was in the till, and contained about 28 s. all in silver, I saw it safe between three and four o'clock; I found the bag still there and 18 d. left in it. The prisoner was servant in the house; we charged her with taking it, she denied it; an officer was called, she was searched, but nothing found. After that, I remembered seeing her with a little box the day before, and asked her to produce it. She at last produced it out of a sack of sawdust which was kept in the kitchen, and begged of me to forgive her, and at the same time gave me 21 s., saying she had not taken a sixpence more.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. You said if she confessed, you would not hurt her - A. It was said so at first, that if she would give up the money, Mr. Smith would do nothing.
THOMAS HARRISON. I am an officer. I was in the parlour at the time Smith complained of money being taken. I searched the prisoner, but found nothing. Smith said if she gave up the money, he would forgive her, but as she did not do so, the bar-maid said she had a box; she was asked about it, and said she had given it to a young girl; the girl denied it; she fell down and said,
"What shall I do?" I said, tell where the property is, and your master will forgive you. She said, it was in the sawdust box, and produced it from there.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18230910-316]
1286. EDWARD HOGAN was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of August, a handkerchief, value 2 s., the goods of William Sandford, from his person.
WILLIAM SANDFORD. I am a journeyman silversmith, and live in Monkwell-street. On the 3d of August, about ten o'clock at night, I was passing along Bloomsbury, and heard the cry of Stop thief! I saw somebody running along the other side of the way; Riley brought the prisoner up to me, and said, he had picked my pocket; I then felt, and missed my handkerchief. Riley produced it.
PHILIP RILEY. I am a patrol of Bloomsbury. About ten o'clock at night, I saw the prisoner and two others at the corner of George-street, I watched them; the prosecutor and a female went by - they both followed behind; the prisoner stopped down, and took the handkerchief out of his pocket, turned round and ran against me; I seized him, and said,
"Ned, this won't do;" he immediately dropped it, and I picked it up, and called to the people to stop the prosecutor.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. He laid hold of me, and said I stole the handkerchief - he found it on the ground.
GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Life,
[trialAccount t18230910-317]
1287. ELEANOR COLLINS was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of September, a watch, value 20 s., the goods of James Lampard, from his person.
JAMES LAMPARD. I am a hair-dresser. On the 8th of September, about ten o'clock in the morning, I went to a public-house, in Golden-lane; I was a little fresh - I had got up about seven o'clock; the prisoner was at the public-house with her mother and two other women. I treated them, and when they were gone, I missed my watch which was safe when I went into their company - they all sat by me at times - I found it in pawn. I never lost my senses, and knew who was with me.
DANIEL POPE. I am a pawnbroker in Whitecross-street. The prisoner pawned the watch with me. I am certain of her.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I never pawned it or stole it - the person who did it has been to me in the prison.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-318]
1288. CHARLOTTE ANN ALVEY was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of September, from the person of John Holmes, seven sovereigns, and one Bank note, his property.
JOHN HOLMES. I am a sailor. On the night of the 6th of September. I met the prisoner at the Black Horse, public-house, Rosemary-lane; she asked me to have something to drink - I gave her some rum and water, and stopped there for half an hour; I had a 5 l. note in my waistcoat pocket, and seven sovereigns in my side pocket. I do not know what time it was - I was drunk, but recollect what passed - she put her hand into my pocket, and took seven sovereigns - I felt her do it, and she took the 5 l. note from my waistcoat pocket; we were then in the street, we had gone outside the door - I took the 5 l. note out of her mouth - she ran away, I did not follow her; but in the morning, I found her drinking, and gave her in charge. I gave the note to the officer, and have not found the sovereigns.
THOMAS HARRISON. I am an officer. I was going out on the 7th of September; and at the watch-house, I found the prosecutor, the prisoner, and two more - he gave charge of her for robbing him of seven sovereigns, and a 5 l. note, and shewed me the note, saying he took it from her mouth; he was a little fresh then; I asked her what she did with the sovereigns; she said she did not take them; I asked her what she did with the note - she gave no answer.
Prisoner's Defence. I met him about half past one o'clock at night; he said, he wanted a girl who lived in St. Catherine's. I said, perhaps I might know her - I went to look for her - he had 5 s. or 6 s. worth of rum and water, and was very much in liquor.
GUILTY. Aged 29.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18230910-319]
Before Mr. Recorder.
1289. LAWRENCE CLEAREY was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of July, thirty-nine sovereigns, the monies of Thomas Faby, from his person.
MR. WALFORD conducted the prosecution.
THOMAS FABY. I live in Brewer's-court, Bedfordbury; the prisoner lodged in my house. On the 7th of July, about half-past eleven o'clock, I received thirty-nine sovereigns fourteen shillings and eleven-pence, at the Bank - Mary Knaven was with me. After receiving my money it rained, and we went into the Mansion-house, public-house, and had a pot of ale; I was in her company about two hours and a half, and parted with her in Fleet-street. I had drank three glasses of liquor and part of a pot of ale; when I parted from her, she asked if my money was safe, I felt and found it so. I went home about three o'clock, and went to bed in one of my lodger's rooms, it was the prisoner's room. I cannot say whether my money was then safe, I continued in bed from three to five o'clock; I had carried my money loose in my trowsers pocket; the last time I felt, it was in Fleet-street. I met nobody in the way home, nor did I stay anywhere that I recollect. I awoke at five o'clock, and my money was gone; I saw the prisoner at eleven that night, and saw him again next day, and he shewed me eleven sovereigns in a pocket book; I asked how he came by them, he said he had them from a young woman who he kept company with, and who had taken them from a foreigner overnight.
Cross-examined by MR. PRENDERCAST. Q. Do not youknow that he was acquainted with a young woman - A. He knew a servant at Newton's hotel, St. Martin's-street, which is a house for the reception of foreigners; he said the woman had robbed the foreigner of it - I suspected that it was mine. I am sure it was safe when I left Knaven; I did not charge her with taking it. She was examined at Guildhall, on the 10th or 11th of July, for being in company with me; I could not then recollect when I had the money; it came to my memory next evening; she was two days in custody. I called at Knaven's house; I was not drunk when I went there - I carried her child home; she then came on to Fleet-street with me; I fell down on her stairs, as my foot slipped. I took the prisoner up three weeks after, he had not absconded, but lodged with me all the time. I never consulted a fortune-teller about it.
MR. WALFORD. Q. You fell down at Knaven's house - A. Yes; my money was safe after that. I went to her house to ask about it, that occasioned words between me and her husband. I was taken before the Magistrate, and she was brought up by the Magistrate's desire.
MARY KNAVEN. I am the wife of John Knaven - my husband is the prosecutor's first cousin; he is a bricklayer. I went to the Bank with him - he was sober. He received a great many sovereigns at the Bank, and put them into his breeches pocket; this was past twelve o'clock. He went to a public-house with me, and then went to my house; he drank some liquor, in Bow-lane; he had a pot of ale at a public-house, also three half quarterns of rum, and a quartern of gin - he was tipsy. I went as far as St. Paul's with him, and said,
"Have you got your money safe?"
"Oh! Yes," said he, slipping his hand in his pocket, and I heard his money jingle. I parted with him at St. Paul's. I live in Great Trinity-lane - he went home with me before I parted with him.
Cross-examined. Q. How long were you in custody - A. He came in the afternoon; the constable took me - I was discharged the same day. He told the Magistrate he rather thought I had got it; he had come to my house that night, and said he was sure I had his money, and told the Magistrate so.
MARGARET FABY. I am the wife of the prosecutor. The prisoner lodged with us. On the morning of the robbery he wanted to borrow 2 s. 6 d. of me, to go to a raffle at night; I did not lend it him, and he got me to send my girl to pawn a pair of breeches, for 2 s. 6 d.; she went and brought me the half-crown, between nine and ten o'clock in the morning, and he never came to me for it. My husband came home after three o'clock, and went up and slept in the prisoner's room, till five o'clock. He came into the house while my husband was up stairs; I do not know whether he went up, for I went out, leaving him there. A woman, who lived with the prisoner, as his wife (but not in our house) was in the house at the time.
COURT. Q. I suppose she went into the prisoner's room whenever she came to the house - A. Yes; she and the prisoner had plenty of opportunities of going there - she was in the house between three and five o'clock, and the prisoner was there with her. He owed me 1 l. 15 s. 4 d. for rent, and paid me next morning, and said he got the money from a young woman who robbed a foreigner of it. He shewed me ten or eleven sovereigns in a pocket-book, and said he was to get 20 l. as a fortune with the young woman who he married; he married her two or three days after, and told me he was married by licence.
Cross-examined. Q. When did you see the prisoner at the house - A. About a quarter after three o'clock; I was sitting in my room, and told him my husband had come home. The woman was in my room at the time, both before and after the prisoner came in. The prisoner's room is on the second floor; I went out, leaving them together - I was not absent a quarter of an hour.
MR. WALFORD. Q. How long after your husband went to bed did the woman come in - A. She was in the house a quarter of an hour - her name is Mary Riley - she saw my husband come in and go up stairs. She went up and down before Clearey came in.
JAMES BROWN. I lodge in the house. On this morning the prisoner wanted me to lend him 2 s. 6 d. to go to a raffle - I gave him leave to pawn my breeches for 2 s. 6 d., as I had no money. I saw him again between seven and eight o'clock in the evening at a wine vaults - he changed a sovereign, and paid me. I asked how he got it - he said he got it of his wife.
JOHN DUFFY, I am a labourer, and live in Oxford Buildings. On the 7th of July I saw the prisoner at a raffle in Broad-street, Carnaby-market, and saw him with some sovereigns; he said he had plenty of money, and shewed me a white stocking, and strewed them all over the table; he said he got them by hard labour.
Cross-examined. Q. This was a long time ago - A. I cannot tell - it was not at a raffle - I heard of a raffle that evening.
MARY BAILEY. I keep the Black Horse, public-house, Vine-street, Chandos-street; the prisoner came to my house one day the beginning of July with a woman, and had a glass of rum and water, about ten o'clock in the morning; I changed a sovereign for him - and then changed another for him.
THOMAS GAYNARD. Shortly after I heard of this robbery the prisoner told me he had got a great deal of money from a young woman, who took it from a foreigner. I saw him give a man eleven sovereigns.
Cross-examined. Q. Where was this - A. At Faby's house. The man said he gave him eleven sovereigns; I did not see him with them myself.
THOMAS BOYLE. The prisoner told me he had some sovereigns of a woman who robbed a foreigner of them. I saw him change one at a public-house.
ANTHONY M'KEAL. I saw the prisoner with eleven sovereigns at Faby's house on the Saturday after the robbery; he said he got them from a sweetheart of his - that he went to borrow two shillings of her to go to a raffle, and she gave them to him; and he rather thought she got them from a foreigner.
JOSEPH HOLYLAND. I am an officer. I searched the prisoner's lodging in Faby's house, and found nineteen sovereigns in a box, wrapped in a piece of rag. There were no men's clothes there - only ribbons and caps. A woman who called herself his wife gave us the key, and was present at the search.
THOMAS FABY. I saw the box searched - it belonged to his wife; he married her three days after the robbery. He kept his clothes in that box.
Prisoner's Defence. I did not leave work till half past five o'clock that afternoon, then came home. Faby said her husband was drunk, and told me to go to watch for him - I went and staid till eight o'clock, then returned, and found neither of them at home; they were gone to Knaven's house; he said she had the money. I never denied having this money.
THOMAS BOYLE re-examined. I came home about five o'clock - I do not know where the prisoner was then - he went to watch in Faby's place; I went into the room, and Faby was dressing himself.
MICHAEL NEEREY. I knew the prisoner in Ireland and in England. I saw him with forty-seven sovereigns at the time Faby was robbed; he counted them twice at the raffle, and said he found them between Whitcomb-street and the Haymarket.
MR. WALFORD. Q. Are you sure it was on the day of the raffle - A. Yes; on the day before; it was at the Fortune of War, East-street.
GUILTY. Aged 27.
Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18230910-320]
1290. ANN BURNS was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of June, two crowns, one half-crown, five shillings, and five sixpences, the monies of Margaret Chappell, widow, from her person.
MARGARET CHAPPELL. I live in George-yard, Whitechapel. On the 25th of June, about half-past four o'clock in the afternoon, the prisoner came and asked me to walk with her; we went to a wine vaults, and had a quartern of rum - she wanted me to buy the duplicate of a blanket, but I refused - she said, if she had 1 s. 6 d. she would take it out of pawn. I went home for my money, and put a pound in my bosom, which I had to go to market with. It was two crowns, a half-crown, and silver. I lent her 18 d. and then called for another quartern of rum; I found my head heavy, and fell asleep. A young man awoke me, I found my money was gone; I caught her over the way at another wine vaults, and said,
"Where is my money, that you took out of my bosom;" she said, if I would go to her sister, she would make it all right. I caught hold of her hand, and a half-crown and 1 s. 6 d. fell from it on the ground.
Prisoner. Q. Were you not tried here - A. Never; I was at Hick's-hall for an assault.
JAMES CATHERWOOD. I went into the wine vaults to get some beer, the prosecutrix was asleep. I saw the prisoner with her arm round the prosecutrix's neck, and her hand at her bosom; she pushed the corner of her handkerchief out; I suspected she was robbing her; she untied the corner of the handkerchief. I told the landlady of it, and she awoke the prosecutrix. The prisoner was crossing the road at the time the prosecutor went after her - I did not say anything to her. I am a horse dealer, and live in George-yard, Whitechapel.
JOHN SEABROOK. I was attending at the opposite wine vaults. The prisoner came in, and asked me to take care of 15 s. for her; I have known her a long time - she had scarcely put it down before the prosecutrix followed her, and seized her by the collar; she directly said,
"If you will go to my sister's, I will make it all right with you;" they went out together. She has put money in my care before.
FRANCIS FREEMAN. On the 25th of June, the prosecutrix brought the prisoner to the office. She was discharged at the second examination, as we did not know the money was left with Seabrook. I found two sixpences on her.
Prisoner's Defence. I received 30 s. on the 24th, I was coming home, and met the prosecutrix, and went to drink with her; she asked me to lend her some money, as she had lost a good deal by mackarel. I said, I had none to lend her - we drank two or three hours. I went to leave my money with this young man; she came over and said, I had robbed her, and took 2 s. 6 d. out of my hand.
JOHN SEABROOK. The prisoner said nothing at my house about the prosecutrix having no money.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18230910-321]
1291. GEORGE WALDUCK was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of June, five jugs, value 5 s.; six dishes, value 3 s.; two chamber pots, value 18 d.; twelve cups, value 18 d.; an ewer, value 18 d., and a basin, value 10 d., the goods of Sarah Goodman.
SARAH GOODMAN. I live in Walbrook-place, Hoxton. I employed men to hawk earthenware. The prisoner came to live with me on the 21st of May. On the 3d of June I sent him out with a basket of things - he never returned; he had given me no notice. There were altogether twenty-six shillings worth,
WILLIAM CONSTANTINE. I apprehended the prisoner at Pickford's wharf, in the City-road. On the 14th of July, the prosecutrix fetched me to take him; I told him the charge; he said he would go and work it out.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-322]
1292. JOSEPH HOPE was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of September, a seal, value 30 s.; a watch key, value 15 s., and a ring, value 1 s., the goods of Richard Price, from his person.
RICHARD PRICE. I keep the Magpie and Stump, public-house, in the New-road. St. George's in the East . On the 6th of September, between five and six o'clock in the afternoon, I was in my bar - the prisoner came and asked for a glass of gin - he went into the tap-room; I took it to him; he said,
"This won't do for me, I want my gin in a measure;" I put it into a measure, and gave it to him, and asked him for the money - he gave me 1 1/2 d.; I said I wanted 2 d.; he said he should give me no more; I insisted on having 2 d., and said I should turn him out if he did not pay me. He was going to sit down; I took and carried him to the outside door; he immediately made a short turn, and snatched at my watch; the ring broke, and he set off with the seal, and I followed and secured him about twenty yards off. He scuffled a good deal - the seal was found just by; he had thrown it into the road while I pursued him.
JOHN CHURCHILL. I am servant to Mr. Price. I picked up the seal three minutes after he was secured; it was eight or ten yards from the door.
(Seal produced and sworn to.)
FRANCIS JACKSON. I am a constable. I found the prisoner struggling with Price - he said he was surgeon of a frigate.
The prisoner in a written Defence, stated that he was inebriated,and in the scuffle he happened to catch hold of the watch, and the ribbon broke; but denied any felonious intention.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-323]
1293. ANN HALL was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of August, a watch, value 2 l.; a watch key, value 3 s.; a seal, value 6 s.; a chain, value 6 d.; a pocket-book, value 6 d., and two handkerchiefs, value 1 s., the goods of Robert Smeaton.
ROBERT SMEATON. I belong to the Revenue service. On the 30th of August, about eleven o'clock at night, I went into a court in Cable-street, for a necessary purpose, and the prisoner came up the court, and invited me into her room in the same court - another woman came for 2 s. for the use of the room - I stopped till four o'clock next morning. The room door was locked - my watch was under the bolster, and my trowsers and property also. I missed my watch, and accused her of taking it - she denied it. I was perfectly sober.
FRANCIS JACKSON. I am a constable. On Sunday morning, the 31st of August between four and five o'clock, the watchman brought the prisoner to the watch-house - she had left the house. I went to the room between eleven and twelve o'clock, and found the handkerchief, with carrots and turnips tied up in it. I found the prisoner in bed there, with a man she lives with; she was much intoxicated, and the prosecutor was rather fresh. She said she gave the watch to another girl, or that another girl had it. There is no lock to the door.
Prisoner's Defence. I met him very drunk; he sent me out for drink, and when I came back he was asleep, and the door open. I awoke him, and he drank most of the liquor. Another woman came up, and drank with him.
ROBERT SMEATON. There was no liquor in the room. I thought she locked the door.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-324]
1294. JOHN WILLIAMS and WILLIAM HIGGS were indicted, for stealing, on the 26th of August, a waistcoat, value 2 s., and a piece of cloth, value 1 s., the goods of William Crow, from his person.
WILLIAM CROW. At the time of the robbery, I lived at Ashford, near Staines. On the 26th of August, about eleven o'clock at night, I was coming from Hounslow to Ashford, I was not intoxicated - I had a bundle, containing a waistcoat and an apron. There were four young men on the road in liquor; they snatched my bundle from me; the prisoners are two of them; they snatched the bundle out of my hand. I knew two of them before, but not the prisoners - Page, who I knew, snatched it and gave it to Williams, he delivered it up to me, and Page snatched it from me again; I got the waistcoat back the same night It might be a joke.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-325]
1295. RICHARD LOVELL was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of September, one shilling, one sixpence, and five penny pieces, the monies of Maria Gilham, from her person.
MARIA GILHAM. I am an unfortunate girl. I lived in Twine-court, Shadwell. Last Friday fortnight, about half-past eight o'clock at night, the prisoner came into my room - I had seen him in the street before - I refused to give him my company - he insisted on it, and put the light out, and pushed me on the bed - while I was on the bed, he accused me of stealing a five shilling piece from him. I denied it - he then said, I had stolen half-a-crown. I said, I had not got one - he turned up the bed, and said he knew where the money was, and took a shilling, a sixpence and five penny pieces from under the bed; he said, it was part of his money. I knew him before: he ill used me, and I charged him with an assault.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-326]
1296. SOPHIA FOX was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of September, a pair of boots, value 6 s., the goods of Joseph Hodges.
JOSEPH HODGES. I am a shoemaker, and live in Mary-le-bone-lane. On the 11th of September, about half-past six o'clock in the evening, these boots were stolen. I went out at ten minutes after six, and they were safe - I returned and found the prisoner in custody with them.
JOHN CHUMLEY. I live opposite to Hodges. About half-past six o'clock in the evening, I saw the prisoner go up to the prosecutor's door, and take these goods down, and walk away with them; I ran over and called Mrs. Hodges, who pursued, and took her with them.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
SARAH HODGES. I followed her and took them from her.
GUILTY.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18230910-327]
1297. CHARLES FULLER was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of July, three hundred nails, value 18 d., the goods of Mark Cohen.
MARK COHEN. I am a builder, and live in John-street, Bedford-square. On the 10th of July, about eight o'clock in the morning, I filled a box with six hundred nails, and left them in care of a boy, while I was sent for to breakfast; I returned in half an hour, and missed half of them. The prisoner was employed as a plaisterer at some buildings in the neighbourhood - he was taken four or five days after, and brought to me - my boy is not here.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-328]
1298. ROBERT GOODISON was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of September, five loaves of bread, value 3 s. 6 d., the goods of James Vyse.
JAMES VYSE. I am a baker. On the 11th of September I sent the prisoner out with sixty-four loaves and a half of bread to serve the customers with. He brought ten and a half home, and booked all the rest to customers, but has not accounted to me for the money.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18230910-329]
1299. RICHARD PHILLIPS was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of September, four books, value 10 s., the goods of William Bowles.
WILLIAM BOWLES, JUN. I am the son of William Bowles, a bookseller, and live in the New-road, Somers-town. These books were on a board in front of the shop. I saw the prisoner at the board, and in consequence of what I heard, I went and overtook him with four books which are my father's. He pleaded distress.
MARY RICE. I saw him go from this shop with something, and told Bowles, who found the books on him.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18230910-330]
1300. JOHN QUIN was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of September, a handkerchief, value 5 s., the goods of Michael Wallis.
SOPHIA VICKERS. I have a house in Glasshouse-fields; Michael Wallis rents a room in it. The prisoner came to the door, and asked for Wallis. I said,
"He is not within" This handkerchief laid on the table, in the same room. I went down into the cellar while he was there, and when I came up he was gone with it. I went after him, and took him just by. He gave it me out of his pocket.
JOHN GRIFFITHS. I am surveyor of the roads. I was near the prosecutor's premises. He gave an alarm that the prisoner had robbed him. I followed, and brought him back. I asked how he came to rob a poor seaman. He said the devil tempted him.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 31.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18230910-331]
1301. MARY THON was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of July, a shirt, value 2 s.; a petticoat, value 1 s.; a bonnet, value 5 s., and a frock, value 6 d., the goods of Michael Deady; and three caps, value 1 s. the goods of Ellen Connel.
MICHAEL DEADY. I am a labourer, and live in Calmell-buildings. On the 24th of July I got up to go to work. Between five and six o'clock I went down to open my bed-room door, to call my fellow-labourer. I opened the door, and met the prisoner coming out. She turned to go down stairs. I stopped her, and asked what brought her there. All this property was in the room. I said she should not go unless she said what brought her into my room. I found three boxes turned over, and property taken out. The things in question, which were safe overnight, were packed up, to be taken away. My fellow-labourer was fast asleep in the room. She said a woman in the street had sent her. Three caps of Connell's, who lodges with me, were found in her pocket.
HENRY STEEL. I am an officer. I went to the house, and found her there. She would not say a word. The property was tied up in a bundle.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I met one Murphy, who said she lodged in this house, but owed a little money, and they would not let her have her things, and sent me for them.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
